   [MATH: <semantics><mi>f</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   as
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">:=</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⊂</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>ρ</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>S</mi></msup></mrow></mrow></mro
   w><mo rspace="1.337em">,</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">T_{\rho}f(x):=\sum_{S\subset[n]}{\widehat{
   f}(S)\rho^{|S|}x^{S}},\quad\,x\in\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\,.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   An important feature of this operator is presented in the next result
   of Bonami and Gross (see, e.g., [21, Chapter 9]): For any
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo><</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>
   ≤</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">1<p\leq
   q\leq\infty</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mo>≤</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>
   −</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>q</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfr
   ac></msqrt></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\rho\leq\sqrt{\frac{p-1}{q-1}}</annotation
   ></semantics> :MATH]
   ,
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>f</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi>q</mi></msub><mo>≤</mo><msub><mrow><m
   o stretchy="false">‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi>p</mi></msub></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\|T_{\rho}f\|_{q}\leq\|f\|_{p}.</annotatio
   n></semantics> :MATH]

   The following consequence will be crucial: For for every degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   function
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   (22)
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>‖</mo>
   </mrow><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><msqrt><mrow><mi>p</m
   i><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msqrt></mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">)</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mrow><mo>‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>‖</mo></mrow><mi
   >p</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mn>   1</mn><mo><</mo><mi>
   p</mi><mo>≤</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\left\|f\right\|_{2}\leq\big(1/\sqrt{p-1}\
   big)^{d}\,\left\|f\right\|_{p}\,\,,\,\,\,1<p\leq
   2,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   and
   (23)
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>‖</mo></mrow
   ><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msup><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mrow><mo>‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>‖</mo></mrow><mn
   >1</mn></msub></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\left\|f\right\|_{2}\leq
   e^{d}\,\left\|f\right\|_{1}\,.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

2.3. Polarization I

   We will make use of the following polarization formula (inspired by its
   complex analog due to Harris [14, Theorem 1]).

Proposition 2.2.

   Let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">P:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</ann
   otation></semantics> :MATH]
   be a
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous polynomial, and
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">L:(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R
   }</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   its (unique) associated
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -linear symmetric form. Then for every
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mr
   ow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1\leq k\leq
   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><m
   row><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow></mrow><annotatio
   n
   encoding="application/x-tex">x,y\in[-1,1]^{n}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mover><mover
   accent="true"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏞</mo></mover><mi>k</mi></mover><mo>,</mo><mover><mover
   accent="true"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏞</mo></mover><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mro
   w></mover><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msub><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi
   >k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>d</mi><mi>d</mi></msup><mrow><msup>
   <mi>k</mi><mi>k</mi></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mro
   w></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>!</mo></mrow>
   <mo lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>!</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><
   mo>!</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><mi>P</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></msub></mrow></mrow><mo
   >,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">|L(\overbrace{x,\ldots,x}^{\mbox{$k$}},\ov
   erbrace{y,\ldots,y}^{\mbox{$d-k$}})|\leq
   M_{k,d}\,\frac{d^{d}}{k^{k}(d-k)^{d-k}}\,\frac{k!\,(d-k)!}{d!}\,\|P\|_{
   [-1,1]^{n}}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   where
   [MATH:
   <semantics><msub><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><
   /msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">M_{k,d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is the Markov number given in (12).

Proof.

   Let
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><m
   row><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow></mrow><annotatio
   n
   encoding="application/x-tex">x,y\in[-1,1]^{n}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   . Consider the degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   (real) polynomial
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo>⁡</m
   o><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mr
   ow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">g(t)=P\left(t\,k\,x+(d-k)\,y\right).</anno
   tation></semantics> :MATH]

   Using the multinomial formula, we write
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mi>j</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mi>j</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>k</mi><mi>j</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>j</mi></mro
   w></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mover><mover
   accent="true"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏞</mo></mover><mi>j</mi></mover><mo>,</mo><mover><mover
   accent="true"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏞</mo></mover><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>j</mi></mro
   w></mover><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><an
   notation
   encoding="application/x-tex">g(t)=\sum_{j=0}^{d}{\binom{d}{j}\,t^{j}k^{
   j}(d-k)^{d-j}\,L(\overbrace{x,\ldots,x}^{\mbox{$j$}},\overbrace{y,\ldot
   s,y}^{\mbox{$d-j$}})},</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   and hence by Markov’s inequality (13) we deduce that
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msup><mi>k</mi><mi>k</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mro
   w></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mover><mover
   accent="true"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏞</mo></mover><mi>k</mi></mover><mo>,</mo><mover><mover
   accent="true"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏞</mo></mover><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mro
   w></mover><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msub><mi>M</mi><m
   row><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub><mo lspace="0.167em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">sup</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msub><mi>M
   </mi><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub><mo lspace="0em"

   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><mi>P</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotatio
   n
   encoding="application/x-tex">k^{k}(d-k)^{d-k}\binom{d}{k}|L(\overbrace{
   x,\ldots,x}^{\mbox{$k$}},\overbrace{y,\ldots,y}^{\mbox{$d-k$}})|\leq
   M_{k,d}\,\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}{|g(t)|}\leq
   M_{k,d}\,d^{d}\|P\|,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   which leads to the desired conclusion. ∎

2.4. Proof of the homogeneous case

   For the proof of (16) let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   be an
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous function with
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d\geq 2</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   (the case
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d=1</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is anyway clear). We rewrite it in the form (18),
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a<
   /mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></mro
   w><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f(x)=\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{J}(d,n)}{
   a_{\mathbf{j}}x_{\mathbf{j}}}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   where
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><
   /mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{\mathbf{j}}=0</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   whenever
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>𝐣</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{j}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is not strictly increasing, and extend the definition of the
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{\mathbf{j}}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   to all of
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{I}(d,n)</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   by
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>𝐢</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>a
   </mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{\mathbf{i}}=a_{\mathbf{j}}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   if
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>𝐣</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}\in[\mathbf{j}]</annotation></se
   mantics> :MATH]
   . Moreover, for the coefficients
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>𝐢</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">c_{\mathbf{i}}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   of the unique symmetric form
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">L_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   from (21) we have
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>𝐢</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msu
   b><mi>a</mi><mi>𝐢</mi></msub><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>!</mo></mrow></mfrac><
   /mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">c_{\mathbf{i}}=\frac{a_{\mathbf{i}}}{d!}</
   annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . Then by Proposition 2.1 (
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>⊕</mo><mi>𝐣</mi></
   mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{\mathbf{i}\oplus\mathbf{j}}=0</annotati
   on></semantics> :MATH]
   whenever
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>𝐢</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   or
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>𝐣</mi><annotation

   ow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm
   1\}}^{=k}\,\Big(1+\frac{2}{k-1}\Big)^{\frac{d-k}{2}}(1+\sqrt{2})^{k}\,\
   ,\frac{d^{k}}{k^{k}}\Big(1+\frac{k}{d-k}\Big)^{d-k}</annotation></seman
   tics> :MATH]
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msubsup><m

   where we have used the fact that
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mrow><mo

   [MATH: <semantics><mi>e</mi><annotation

   for positive
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . Taking
   [MATH:

   on
   encoding="application/x-tex">k\approx\sqrt{d/\log{d}}</annotation></sem
   antics> :MATH]
   and using that
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo

   [MATH: <semantics><mi>k</mi><annotation

   , we obtain for some
   [MATH:

   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm

   semantics> :MATH]

   Iterating this process yields
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo

   d</mi></mrow></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm
   1\}}^{=d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msup><mi>e</mi><
   mrow><mo maxsize="1.710em" minsize="1.710em">(</mo><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msqrt><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo

   lspace="0em"

   o lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msqrt><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo

   n encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\leq

   qrt{2}}\sqrt{\log{d}}\big)}\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm

   [MATH:

   Finally, note that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo

   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm

   which is a consequence of (8) and (10) . ∎

3. The degree-
[MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
case

   Equation (16) is a result on
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous tetrahedral polynomials, but we want to pass now to the
   case of degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   functions. Let us recall that in the complex case this step is easily
   done via the trick that was described after (4), but which is not
   available in the real setting. Another way (which also works in the
   complex case) is to split the Fourier expansion of the degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   function
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>f</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   into its
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogenous parts
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f_{m}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   so that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">(</mo><mrow><munder><mo lspace="0em"
   movablelimits="false" rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></munder><msup
   ><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac></msup></mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">)</mo></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</
   mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></msup><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msu
   p><mi>C</mi><msqrt><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo lspace="0.167em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></msqrt></msup><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow
   ><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Big(\sum_{|S|\leq
   d}{|\widehat{f}(S)|^{\frac{2d}{d+1}}}\Big)^{\frac{d+1}{2d}}\leq
   C^{\sqrt{d\log{d}}}\sum_{m=0}^{d}{\|f_{m}\|_{\infty}}\,,</annotation></
   semantics> :MATH]

   and then try to control the norm of
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f_{m}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   by the norm of the full function
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>f</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . However, the best estimation we know for tetrahedral polynomials
   comes from the general estimation from Lemma 1.3 (4) valid for every
   real polynomial. But this constant in fact has exponential growth on
   the degree (in contrast to the complex case in which one gets constant
   [MATH: <semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   by Lemma 1.3 (1)). One may wonder whether this constant can be improved
   in the tetrahedral case. But considering the majority function
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Maj</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0.278em" rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.337em">,</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Maj</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><
   mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>sign</mi><mo>⁡
   </mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo
   ><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>d</mi><
   /msub><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\Maj_{d}:\{\pm 1\}^{d}\rightarrow\{\pm
   1\},\,\Maj_{d}(x)=\sign(x_{1}+\ldots,x_{d})</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   one can easily check that the norm of its
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous part (
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   odd) satisfies
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>Maj</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>m</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><
   mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><
   mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>
   2</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>2</mn><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo
   >−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mfrac><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mfrac><
   mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo
   >)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><
   mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>
   2</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mi>d</mi></msqrt><mi>m</mi></mfrac></
   mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0.170em">,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\|(\Maj_{d})_{m}\|_{\{\pm
   1\}^{d}}=\binom{\frac{d-1}{2}}{\frac{m-1}{2}}\frac{d}{m}\frac{1}{2^{d-1
   }}\binom{d-1}{\frac{d-1}{2}}\geq\binom{\frac{d-1}{2}}{\frac{m-1}{2}}\fr
   ac{\sqrt{d}}{m}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   so taking
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><annota
   tion encoding="application/x-tex">m=(d-1)/2</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   we have
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>Maj</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>m</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mn>2</mn><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>/</mo>
   <mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msqrt><mi>d</mi></msqrt></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\|(\Maj_{d})_{m}\|_{\infty}=\Omega(2^{d/2}
   /\sqrt{d})</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . See the problem posed in section 4.3.

   All of these problems force us to add to the classical strategy,
   described in the preceding section, some apparently new techniques in
   order to prove that in the tetrahedral case we can still get constants
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mrow><mphantom></mphant
   om><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}^{\leq
   d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   that are subexponential as in the complex case (5). In contrast to
   Proposition 1.2 the key is that now Fourier analysis on the Boolean
   cube
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\{\pm 1\}^{n}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   is at our disposal – as in the complex case we have the polytorus
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mi>𝕋</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbb{T}^{n}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   .

3.1. Multiaffine forms

   Let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</ann
   otation></semantics> :MATH]
   be a polynomial of degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . Recall that
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>Q</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   admits a unique representation known as its monomial expansion: There
   is a unique family of scalars
   [MATH:
   <semantics><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mrow></msub><anno
   tation encoding="application/x-tex">a_{j_{1}\ldots
   j_{m}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   such that for each
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><
   /mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}</annotation></semantics
   > :MATH]
   (24)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><
   mn>1</mn></msub><mo>≤</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><
   mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><m
   i>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></m
   sub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></m
   sub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></m
   sub></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{d}{\sum_{1\leq
   j_{1}\leq\ldots\leq j_{m}\leq n}{a_{j_{1}\ldots
   j_{m}}x_{j_{1}}x_{j_{2}}\ldots x_{j_{m}}}}\,.</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]

   For each
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mr
   ow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq m\leq
   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   , the
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous part of
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>Q</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is given then by
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><
   mn>1</mn></msub><mo>≤</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><
   mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><m
   i>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></m
   sub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></m
   sub></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.017em">,</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n<
   /mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{m}(x)=\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\leq\ldots\leq
   j_{m}\leq n}{a_{j_{1}\ldots j_{m}}x_{j_{1}}\ldots x_{j_{m}}},\hskip
   8.53581ptx\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   We say that
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>Q</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is a tetrahedral polynomial if it has the form
   (25)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><
   mn>1</mn></msub><mo><</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo><</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><
   mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><m
   i>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></m
   sub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></m
   sub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></m
   sub></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.307em">,</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n<
   /mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{d}{\sum_{1\leq
   j_{1}<\ldots<j_{m}\leq n}{a_{j_{1}\ldots j_{m}}x_{j_{1}}x_{j_{2}}\ldots
   x_{j_{m}}}},\hskip
   11.38109ptx\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   In other words, tetrahedral polynomials are characterized for being
   affine (a linear map plus a constant) in each variable
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><mi>ℝ</mi><
   /mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">x_{j}\in\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics
   > :MATH]
   . This is equivalent to say that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(x)</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   preserves convex combinations in each variable, i.e., if
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>𝐱</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>𝐱</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><
   /mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{n}</anno
   tation></semantics> :MATH]
   are independent (real) random variables, then
   (26)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>𝔼</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>𝐱</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>𝐱</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><
   mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</m
   o><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>𝔼</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><msub><mi>𝐱</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>𝔼</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><m
   o stretchy="false">[</mo><msub><mi>𝐱</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbb{E}[Q(\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf
   {x}_{n})]=Q(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{1}],\ldots,\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{n}
   ]).</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   As we mentioned in the introduction, the importance of these
   polynomials for us is that there is a natural (isometric)
   correspondence between them and real-valued functions on the Boolean
   cube. Indeed, if for a given function
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   we put
   (27)
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub
   ><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mrow></msub><mo>:
   =</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo rspace="0.850em"
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mtext> where </mtext><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo
   ><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo><</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo><</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><
   mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mn> 0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</
   mi><mo lspace="0.170em">,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{j_{1}\ldots
   j_{m}}:=\widehat{f}(S)\hskip 8.53581pt\mbox{ where }\hskip
   8.53581ptS=\{j_{1}<\ldots<j_{m}\},\quad\,0\leq m\leq
   d\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   then the Fourier-Walsh expansion (1) of
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>f</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   can be rewritten as
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><
   mn>1</mn></msub><mo><</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo><</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><
   mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><m
   i>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></m
   sub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></m
   sub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>j</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></m
   sub></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.307em">,</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{d}{\sum_{1\leq
   j_{1}<\ldots<j_{m}\leq n}{a_{j_{1}\ldots j_{m}}x_{j_{1}}x_{j_{2}}\ldots
   x_{j_{m}}}},\hskip 11.38109ptx\in\{\pm
   1\}^{n}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   We denote by
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   the unique tetrahedral polynomial which coincides with
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>f</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   on
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\{\pm 1\}^{n}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   , with their respective monomial and Fourier coefficients related via
   (27).

   Following [17, pp. 1065–66], every polynomial
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</ann
   otation></semantics> :MATH]
   of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   has a unique
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -form
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0.278em" rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}:(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{d}\rightarrow\math
   bb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   satisfying the following properties:
    1. (I)
       [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       is (separately)
       [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       -affine, i.e., it is an affine map in each variable
       [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>
       n</mi></msup></mrow><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">x^{(j)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}</annotation><
       /semantics> :MATH]
       .
    2. (II)
       [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       is symmetric, i.e., the value of
       [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo
       lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi
       mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}(x^{(1)},\ldots,x^{(d)})</annotat
       ion></semantics> :MATH]
       is invariant by permutations of its coordinates
       [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi
       mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">x^{(1)},\ldots,x^{(d)}</annotation></s
       emantics> :MATH]
       .
    3. (III)
       [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo
       lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
       mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>
       ⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}(x,\ldots,x)=Q(x)</annotation></s
       emantics> :MATH]
       for every
       [MATH:
       <semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></ms
       up></mrow><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}</annotation></seman
       tics> :MATH]
       .

   For each
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</m
   i></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">x^{(1)},\ldots,x^{(d)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}</a
   nnotation></semantics> :MATH]
   this form can be explicitly written via the polarization formula
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo fence="true" lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">CLOSE</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle
   L_{Q}(x^{(1)},</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mo fence="true"
   rspace="0em">OPEN</mo><mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\ldots,x^{(d)})</annotation><
   /semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>𝔼
   </mi><mi>ξ</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mstyle

   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>+</mo><mrow><msub><mi>ξ</mi><mi>d</mi></
   msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow></mrow></m
   row><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[\sum_{
   m=0}^{d}{\frac{\xi_{1}\ldots\xi_{d}}{d!}(\xi_{1}+\ldots+\xi_{n})^{d-m}Q
   _{m}{(\xi_{1}x^{(1)}+\ldots+\xi_{d}x^{(d)})}}\right],</annotation></sem
   antics> :MATH]

   where we are considering expectation over all
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>ξ</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\xi\in\{\pm
   1\}^{d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . Let us notice a couple of observations:
    1. (i)
       If
       [MATH: <semantics><mi>Q</mi><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">Q</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       is
       [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       -homogeneous, then
       [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       is the usual
       [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       -linear form associated to it (see (21)).
    2. (ii)
       For
       [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
       rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
       lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
       stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
       stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
       1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       we will denote by
       [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">L_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       the
       [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       -form associated to its tetrahedral polynomial
       [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       . This form also appears in computer science under the name of
       (fully) decoupled version of
       [MATH: <semantics><mi>f</mi><annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
       and block multilinear form; see e.g [24].

3.2. Polarization II

   The following apparently new result will be crucial for us. It is an
   analog of Harris’ polarization formula for complex homogeneous
   polynomials and its real variant from Proposition 2.2. Our case is much
   more limited, since for a degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   polynomial
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>Q</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   its associated multiaffine form
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   in each variable just preserves convex combinations and not linear
   ones. This forces us to look for a substantially different approach.

Proposition 3.1.

   Let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</ann
   otation></semantics> :MATH]
   be a polynomial of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">L_{Q}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   its
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   [MATH:

   i><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq m\leq d/2</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   we have that
   (28)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">sup</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>y</mi

   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo

   mrow><mo lspace="0em" maxsize="1.200em" minsize="1.200em"

   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo

   nder accentunder="true"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><mi

   ow></munder><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.200em" minsize="1.200em"
   stretchy="true">|</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>d</mi><mi>m</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">sup</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow></munder><mrow><m
   o stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\sup_{x,y\in[-1,1]^{n}}{\big|L_{Q}(\underb
   race{x,\ldots,x}_{m},\underbrace{y,\ldots,y}_{d-m})\big|}\leq
   2d^{m}\sup_{x\in[-1,1]^{n}}{|Q(x)|}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Before starting the proof, we need an auxiliary result on interpolation
   which, as the classical Markov’s theorem from (13), shows another
   extremal property of the coefficients of the Chebychev polynomials. In
   contrast to Markov’s result, we are going to consider a different basis
   for the space of degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   polynomials. For each
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mr
   ow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq m\leq
   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   let
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo>
   <mi>m</mi></mrow></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msup><mn>2</mn><m

   >m</mi></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</

   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\psi_{d,m}(t)=2^{-d}(1+t)^{m}(1-t)^{d-m}=\

   ation></semantics> :MATH]

Proposition 3.2.

   Let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(t)</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   be a degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   polynomial. Then there exist unique scalars
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub
   ><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{n}=a_{n}(Q)</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>≤</mo><m
   i>d</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">(0\leq n\leq d)</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   such that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow>
   <mi>d</mi></munderover><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi>
   </mrow></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.337em">,</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mro
   w><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(t)=\sum_{n=0}^{d}{a_{n}\,\psi_{d,n}(t)},
   \quad\,t\in\mathbb{R}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Moreover, for each
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mr
   ow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq n\leq
   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   (29)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0.337em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">sup</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo rspace="0.170em"
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><an
   notation
   encoding="application/x-tex">|a_{n}(Q)|\leq|a_{n}(T_{d})|\,\sup_{t\in[-
   1,1]}{|Q(t)|}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   where
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">T_{d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is the Chebyshev polynomial of degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . In particular, we have the optimal estimation
   (30)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">≤</mo><mrow><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow>
   <mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow
   ><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow></munderover><mrow><msup><mn>4</mn
   ><mi>m</mi></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><m
   i>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo
   rspace="0.055em">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>⋅</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   lspace="0.055em" movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">sup</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">|a_{n}(Q)|\leq\sum_{m=0}^{\min{\{d-n,n\}}}
   {4^{m}\binom{d}{2m}\binom{d-2m}{n-m}}\cdot\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}{|Q(t)|}.</a
   nnotation></semantics> :MATH]

Proof.

   We start proving that every degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   polynomial
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(t)</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is a linear combination of the elements
   [MATH:
   <semantics><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><
   /msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\psi_{d,m}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   (
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mr
   ow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq m\leq
   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   ) which authomatically will yield the uniqueness of the coefficients by
   basic linear algebra. In the process, we will give an explicit
   expression of these coefficients which will let us prove the inequality
   (29), and finally we will calculate explicitely the coefficients
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{n}(T_{d})</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   to conclude from (29) that (30) holds.

   We start by fixing
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d+1</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   arbitrary points (although we will choose them suitably later)
   (31)
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>≤</mo><msub
   ><mi>t</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo><</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub>
   <mo><</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo><</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><
   mo>≤</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">-1\leq t_{0}<t_{1}<\ldots<t_{d}\leq
   1.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Recall that the associated Lagrange basis polynomials are given by
   (32)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi
   mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∏</mo><mtable><mtr><mtd><mrow><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo
   >=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>≠</mo><mi>m</mi><
   /mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr></mtable><mi>d</mi></munderover><mfrac><mrow><
   mi>t</mi><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><msub>
   <mi>t</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><
   /mrow></mfrac></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="1.017em">,</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><m
   i>d</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta_{m}(t)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=
   0,j\neq m\end{subarray}}^{d}{\frac{t-t_{j}}{t_{m}-t_{j}}}\,,\hskip
   8.53581pt0\leq m\leq d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   which satisfy that
   (33)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow>
   <mi>d</mi></munderover><mrow><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi
   mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.017em">,</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(t)=\sum_{m=0}^{d}{Q(t_{m})\Delta_{m}(t)}
   ,\hskip 8.53581ptt\in[-1,1].</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Now, we are going to write the polynomials
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi
   mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta_{m}(t)</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   in terms of the
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi></

   . Note that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munderover><mo

   encoding="application/x-tex">\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0,j\neq
   m\end{subarray}}^{d}{(t-t_{j})}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0,j\neq
   m\end{subarray}}^{d}{\left(\left(\frac{1+t}{2}\right)\left(1-t_{j}\righ
   t)-\left(\frac{1-t}{2}\right)\left(1+t_{j}\right)\right)}.</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]

   [MATH:

   (36)

   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(t)=\sum_{n=0}^{d}{\left(\sum_{m=0}^{d}{Q

   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><annotation

   This proves the first part of the theorem. Our aim now is to show that
   (29) holds. Let us notice first that the
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation

   ’s are ordered according to (31), hence
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munder><mo

   [MATH:

   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . Hence by (38)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo

Proof of Proposition 3.1.

   Fix
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo>∈</mo><

   . Notice that since
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation

   is affine in each variable, for every
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo

   we have that
   (40)

   encoding="application/x-tex">\begin{split}P(t)&:=Q\left(\frac{1+t}{2}x+

   at a convex combination of elements in
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mrow><mo

   , thus
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munder><mo
