   encoding="application/x-tex">\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}{|P(t)|}\leq\|Q\|_{\infty
   }.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Without loss of generality we can assume that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotatio
   n
   encoding="application/x-tex">\|Q\|_{\infty}=1</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   . An application of Theorem 3.2 to (40) gives that, if
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mi>d</m
   i><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq m\leq d/2</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   , then we have the bound
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em"
   stretchy="true">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><munder><munder
   accentunder="true"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏟</mo></munder><mi>m</mi></munder><mo>,</mo><munder><mu
   nder accentunder="true"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="true">⏟</mo></munder><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>m</mi></mr
   ow></munder><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.200em" minsize="1.200em"
   stretchy="true">|</mo></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo
   >(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfra
   c><mo lspace="0.337em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow>
   <mi>m</mi></munderover><mrow><msup><mn>4</mn><mi>k</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>−</mo><m
   i>k</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\big|L_{Q}(\underbrace{x,\ldots,x}_{m},\un
   derbrace{y,\ldots,y}_{d-m})\big|\leq\frac{1}{\binom{d}{m}}\,\sum_{k=0}^
   {m}{4^{k}\binom{d}{2k}\binom{d-2k}{m-k}}.</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]

   To estimate the last constant from above we will use the following
   immediate consequence of Stirling approximation formula
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>!</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><m
   o>(</mo><mfrac linethickness="0pt"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mi>k</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow>
   <mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>!</mo></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><
   mfrac><msup><mn>4</mn><mi>k</mi></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msqrt><mi>k</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>!</mo></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.337em">,</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mro
   w><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">(2k)!=\binom{2k}{k}(k!)^{2}\geq\frac{4^{k}
   }{2\sqrt{k}}(k!)^{2},\quad\,k\geq 1.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Then
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfra
   c></mstyle><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow>
   <mi>m</mi></munderover></mstyle><mrow><msup><mn>4</mn><mi>k</mi></msup>
   <mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></mstyle><mo>)</mo></mrow><
   mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>−</mo><m
   i>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></mstyle><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><
   annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\frac{1}{\binom{d}{m}}\sum_{k
   =0}^{m}{4^{k}\binom{d}{2k}\binom{d-2k}{m-k}}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>=</mo><mrow><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow>
   <mi>m</mi></munderover></mstyle><mrow><msup><mn>4</mn><mi>k</mi></msup>
   <mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>!</mo></mrow
   ><mo lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>!</mo></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow
   ><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>!</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>!</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>−</mo><m
   i>k</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>!</mo></mrow></mfrac></mstyle>
   </mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{m}{4^{k}\frac{m!
   \,(d-m)!}{(2k)!\,(m-k)!\,(d-m-k)!}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</
   mo><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow>
   <mi>m</mi></munderover></mstyle><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msqrt><mi>k</mi></msqrt><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mstyle><mo>)</mo></mr
   ow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><mi>k</m
   i></mfrac></mstyle><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annota
   tion encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\leq
   1+\sum_{k=1}^{m}{2\,\sqrt{k}\,\binom{m}{k}\binom{d-m}{k}}</annotation><
   /semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><
   mo lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow>
   <mi>m</mi></munderover></mstyle><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mstyle
   displaystyle="true"><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mstyle><mo>)</mo></mr
   ow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>k</mi></msup></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   >=</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>d</mi><mi>m</mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo>,
   </mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\leq
   2\,\sum_{k=0}^{m}{\binom{m}{k}(d-1)^{k}}=2d^{m},</annotation></semantic
   s> :MATH]

   which completes the proof. ∎

3.3. Proof of the degree-
[MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
-case

   For the proof of Theorem 1.1 we follow again the general strategy from
   section 2 for which we must introduce some more notation. For each
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>ℕ</mi></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">n\in\mathbb{N}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   and each finite
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⊂</mo><mi>ℕ</mi></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">A\subset\mathbb{N}</annotation></semantics
   > :MATH]
   we define the sets
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(A,n)</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>{
   </mo><mi>𝐢</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mo>∪</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext> map </mtext></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>}</mo><
   /mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{i}:A\rightar
   row[n]\cup\{0\}\mbox{ map }\right\},</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{0}(A,n)</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>{
   </mo><mi>𝐣</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mo>∪</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext> non-decreasing
   map </mtext></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>}</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{j}:A\rightar
   row[n]\cup\{0\}\mbox{ non-decreasing map
   }\right\}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   In case
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">A=[d]</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   for some
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>ℕ</mi></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d\in\mathbb{N}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   , we denote the previous sets by
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{I}_{0}(d,n)</annotation></semanti
   cs> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{J}_{0}(d,n)</annotation></semanti
   cs> :MATH]
   repectively. Given
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}(A,n)</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   , we write
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>supp</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>𝐢</mi></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mi>k</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≠</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{supp}{\mathbf{i}}=\{k\colon\
   mathbf{i}(k)\neq 0\}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   to denote the support of
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>𝐢</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . We again consider the following equivalence relation:
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>𝐢</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>∼</mo><msub><mi>𝐢
   </mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}_{1}\sim\mathbf{i}_{2}</annotati
   on></semantics> :MATH]
   in
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{I}_{0}(A,n)</annotation></semanti
   cs> :MATH]
   whenever there is a bijection
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>σ</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>A</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\sigma:A\rightarrow
   A</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   such that
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>𝐢</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub
   ><mi>𝐢</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo lspace="0.222em"
   rspace="0.222em">∘</mo><mi>σ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}_{1}=\mathbf{i}_{2}\circ\sigma</
   annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . For each
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}(A,n)</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   there is a unique
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{J}_{0}(A,n)</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   such that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>𝐢</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{j}\in[\mathbf{i}]</annotation></se
   mantics> :MATH]
   , where
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>𝐢</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">[\mathbf{i}]</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   as above stands for the equivalent class of
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>𝐢</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . This identification let us rewrite the monomial expansion (24) of a
   degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   polynomial
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</ann
   otation></semantics> :MATH]
   as
   (41)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a<
   /mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∏</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>supp</m
   i><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>𝐣</mi></mrow></mrow></munder><msub><mi>x</mi
   ><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow
   ><mo
   rspace="1.587em">,</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n<
   /mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(x)=\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{J}_{0}(d,
   n)}{a_{\mathbf{j}}\,\prod_{k\in\operatorname{supp}{\mathbf{j}}}{x_{\mat
   hbf{j}(k)}}},\hskip
   14.22636ptx\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Recall that tetrahedral polynomials are affine in each variable, which
   using the notation of (41) means that the nonzero coefficients
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{\mathbf{j}}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   must satisfy that
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>𝐣</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{j}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is injective on its support. For that, we will say that an element
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}(A,n)</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   is affine if
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≠</mo><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>⁡</m
   o><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}(k)\neq\mathbf{i}(k^{\prime})</a
   nnotation></semantics> :MATH]
   whenever
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>k</mi><mo>′</mo></
   msup></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>supp</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>𝐢</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">k,k^{\prime}\in\operatorname{supp}{\mathbf
   {i}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   are different; note that then every element of
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>𝐢</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">[\mathbf{i}]</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   is also affine. As a consequence tetrahedral polynomials have a
   monomial expansion of the form
   (42)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mtable
   rowspacing="0pt"><mtr><mtd><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mtex
   t> affine </mtext></mtd></mtr></mtable></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><
   mi>𝐣</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∏</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>supp</m
   i><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>𝐣</mi></mrow></mrow></munder><msub><mi>x</mi
   ><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow
   ><mo
   rspace="1.587em">,</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n<
   /mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q(x)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{j}\i
   n\mathcal{J}_{0}(d,n)\\ \text{ affine
   }\end{subarray}}{a_{\mathbf{j}}\,\prod_{k\in\operatorname{supp}{\mathbf
   {j}}}{x_{\mathbf{j}(k)}}},\hskip
   14.22636ptx\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   On the other hand, the associated
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -affine form of a polynomial
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>Q</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   with monomial expansion (41) is then given by (check I,II,III from
   section 3.1)
   (43)
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo

   where

   is given by
   (44)

   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi

   In particular,
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi

   for

   tion encoding="application/x-tex">\hat{S}:=[d]\setminus

   .

   We are now in disposition of proving the main result of this article.

Proof of Theorem 1.1.

   Fix
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mr
   ow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq m\leq
   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . Let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"

   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo

   1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   be a function of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   the associated tetrahedral polynomial. We know from (42) that it has a
   monomial expansion
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo

   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi

   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a<
   /mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∏</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>supp</m
   i><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>𝐣</mi></mrow></mrow></munder><msub><mi>x</mi
   ><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo

   ><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{f}(x)=\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{J}(d,

   f{j}(k)}}}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   where
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><
   /mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">a_{\mathbf{j}}=0</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   whenever
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>𝐣</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{j}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is not affine; we extend this definition by putting
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>𝐢</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>a
   </mi><mi>𝐣</mi></msub></mrow><annotation

   </semantics> :MATH]
   if
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo

   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{i}\in[\mathbf{j}]</annotation></se

   for
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐢</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi

   ,
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>𝐣</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi

   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{J}_{0}(d,n)</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
    . Let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0.278em" rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo

   bb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   be the
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -affine form of
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   described above. Then for every
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⊂</mo><mrow><mo

   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">S\subset[d]</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   and
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo

   i></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">x^{(1)},\ldots,x^{(d)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}</a
   nnotation></semantics> :MATH]

   encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{|[\mathbf{j}_{1}\oplus\mathbf{j}_{2}

   displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</m
   n></mrow></mfrac></mstyle><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow>
   </mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0.170em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm
   1\}}^{\leq
   m}\exp{\left(2\left(\frac{d}{m+1}+m\log{d}\right)\right)}\,.</annotatio
   n></semantics> :MATH]

   Taking
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><msqrt><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>log</
   mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></msqrt><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">m=[\sqrt{d/\log{d}}]</annotation></semanti
   cs> :MATH]
   in the previous inequality leads to
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mi>
   d</mi></mrow></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm
   1\}}^{\leq d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msubsup><m
   i>BH</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mro
   w><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><msqrt><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>log</
   mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></msqrt><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo lspace="0.167em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>exp</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mrow><m
   n>4</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msqrt><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></msqrt></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.170em">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><an
   notation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{BH}_{\{\pm
   1\}}^{\leq[\sqrt{d/\log{d}}]}\exp{\left(4\sqrt{d\log{d}}\right)}\,,</an
   notation></semantics> :MATH]

   and iterating (as in the proof of (16)) concludes the argument. ∎

4. Remarks and open problems

4.1. Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture

   Theorem 1.1 proves that
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mi>
   d</mi></mrow></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{BH}^{\leq d}_{\{\pm
   1\}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   grows at most subexponetially in the degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   , but it might even be true that it at most grows polynomially, i.e.,
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mi>
   d</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mi>poly</mi><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{BH}^{\leq d}_{\{\pm
   1\}}\leq\operatorname{poly}{d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . In the following we intend to explain how the Bohnenblust-Hille cycle
   of ideas is linked with quantum information theory.

   Given
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>∈</mo><
   mi>ℕ</mi></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">n,k\in\mathbb{N}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   , a function
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>k</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:(\{\pm
   1\}^{n})^{k}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is said to be a multilinear form if it is linear in each input, i.e.,
   for all
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ
   </mi><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}</annotation></seman
   tics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mi>n</mi></msup><
   /mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}</annotation></semantics
   > :MATH]
   (and similarly for the other positions)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo
   ><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo>
   <mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><
   mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><m
   o>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo>,
   </mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><
   mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</m
   o><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></
   msub><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><
   mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0.170em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f(x_{1}+y,x_{2},\ldots,x_{k})=f(x+y,x_{2},
   \ldots,x_{k})+f(y,x_{2},\ldots,x_{k})\,.</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]

   Clearly, any such
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>k</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous Boolean function can be written as
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>k</mi></msup><
   mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>i
   </mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi></
   msub><mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><
   mrow><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub>
   <mn>1</mn></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi></msub>
   <mi>k</mi></msubsup></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="1.307em">,</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mrow
   ><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>k</mi></msup></mrow><mo>∈</mo><m
   sup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo>
   </mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f(x^{1},\ldots,x^{k})=\sum_{1\leq
   i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq n}{a_{i_{1}\ldots i_{k}}x_{i_{1}}^{1}\ldots
   x_{i_{k}}^{k}},\hskip 11.38109ptx^{1},\ldots x^{k}\in\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   and Montanaro proved in [19] that
   (48)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">(</mo><mrow><munder><mo lspace="0em"
   movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>
   1</mn></msub></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi></
   msub><mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow></munder><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn>
   </msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac></msup></mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">)</mo></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</
   mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msup><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msu
   p><mi>k</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annot
   ation encoding="application/x-tex">\Big(\sum_{1\leq
   i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq n}{|a_{i_{1}\ldots
   i_{k}}|^{\frac{2k}{k+1}}}\Big)^{\frac{k+1}{2k}}\leq
   k^{C}\|f\|_{\infty}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   where
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>></mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">C>0</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is an absolute constant. He moreover applies this estimation to XOR
   games in the setting of quantum information theory, as well as to solve
   a very special case of the Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture from [1]:

Conjeture 4.1.

   Every function
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow[-1,1]</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   satisfies that
   (49)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>poly</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>Var</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.448em">≤</mo><mrow><munder><mi>max</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤<
   /mo><mi>j</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></munder><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>⁡</
   mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo rspace="0.170em"
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><an
   notation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{poly}(\operatorname{Var}(f)/
   d)\leq\,\max_{1\leq j\leq
   n}{\operatorname{Inf}_{j}(f)}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   where
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>Var</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">:=</mo><mrow><msub><mo>∑</mo><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>≠</mo
   ><mi mathvariant="normal">∅</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></mrow><an
   notation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{Var}{(f)}:=\sum_{S\neq\empty
   set}{\widehat{f}(S)^{2}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>⁡</mo><mr
   ow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">:=</mo><mrow><msub><mo>∑</mo><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>∈</mo
   ><mi>S</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></mrow><an
   notation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{Inf}_{j}(f):=\sum_{j\in
   S}{\widehat{f}(S)^{2}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   for every
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">j\in[n]</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   .

   If this conjecture were true, it would imply (informally) that all
   quantum query algorithms could be efficiently simulated by classical
   query algorithms on most inputs. It is not clear whether or not there
   is a direct link between this conjecture and the possible fact that the
   best constant in the polynomial Bohnenblust-Hille inequality
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mi>
   d</mi></mrow></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{BH}^{\leq d}_{\{\pm
   1\}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   for degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   polynomials grows polynomially on
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . We highline two facts connecting both questions:

   (i) Following Montanaro’s approach from [19, Corollary 20], if
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mi>
   d</mi></mrow></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{BH}^{\leq d}_{\{\pm
   1\}}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   grows polynomially on
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   , then conjecture 4.1 holds for all functions
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow[-1,1]</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   satisfying that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mi>α</mi></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\widehat{f}(S)\in\{\pm\alpha\}</annotation
   ></semantics> :MATH]
   for every
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   (
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha\geq 0</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   ). Indeed, we have
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Var</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msup><mi>α</mi><m
   n>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>n</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow
   ></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{Var}{(f)}=\alpha^{2}\sum_{m=
   1}^{d}{\binom{n}{m}}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   and for each
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">j\in[n]</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>⁡</
   mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msup><mi>α</mi><m
   n>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><m
   i>m</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mr
   ow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mfrac><msup><mi>α</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>n</mi></
   mfrac><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow>
   <mi>d</mi></munderover><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>n</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow
   ></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{Inf}_{j}{(f)}=\alpha^{2}\sum
   _{m=1}^{d}{\binom{n-1}{m-1}}=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{d}{m\binom
   {n}{m}}\,.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Moreover, using that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>n</mi><mi>d</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>≥<
   /mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mi>d</mi></mfrac><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\binom{n}{d}\geq(\frac{n}{d})^{d}</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   we get
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mi>d</
   mi></mfrac><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></munderover><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>n</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow
   ></mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></mr
   ow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   lspace="0em" movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi

   ow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></msup></mrow><mo>≤</mo><ms
   up><mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">(</mo><mrow><munder><mo lspace="0em"
   movablelimits="false" rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>≠</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∅</mi></mrow></munder><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac></msup></mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">)</mo></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</
   mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></msup><mo>≤</mo><msubsup><
   mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mi>
   d</mi></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha\left[\frac{n}{d}\sum_{m=1}^{d}{\bin
   om{n}{m}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\alpha\left[\sum_{m=1}^{d}{\binom{n}{
   m}}\right]^{\frac{d+1}{2d}}\leq\Big(\sum_{S\neq\emptyset}{|\widehat{f}(
   S)|^{\frac{2d}{d+1}}}\Big)^{\frac{d+1}{2d}}\leq\operatorname{BH}^{\leq
   d}_{\{\pm 1\}}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   All together this implies
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>Var</mi><mo>⁡</mo><msup><mrow><
   mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>m
   ax</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><
   /msub><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>⁡</
   mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>≤</mo><mrow><ms
   up><mi>α</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi

   linethickness="0pt"><mi>n</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow
   ></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">(</mo><msubsup><mi>BH</mi><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mi>
   d</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{\operatorname{Var}(f)^{2}}{\max_{1\l
   eq j\leq
   n}{\operatorname{Inf}_{j}(f)}}\leq\alpha^{2}n\sum_{m=1}^{d}{\binom{n}{m
   }}\leq d\big(\operatorname{BH}^{\leq d}_{\{\pm
   1\}}\big)^{2}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   (ii) By [13] conjecture 4.1 is known to hold whenever we in (49)
   replace
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   by
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mn>2</mn><mi>d</mi></msup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">2^{d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . A short proof of this fact based on hypercontractivity can be found
   in [24], and there is a close resemblance between the main idea of this
   proof and the proofs of Bohnenblust-Hille type inequalities. Indeed,
   given
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow[-1,1]</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   we have that
   (50)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>Var</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">≤</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow>
   <mi>n</mi></munderover><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>⁡</
   mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msqrt><mro
   w><munder><mi>max</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi
   >n</mi></mrow></munder><mo
   lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>⁡</
   mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></msqrt><mo
   lspace="0.337em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow>
   <mi>n</mi></munderover><msqrt><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub>
   <mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></msqrt></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{Var}{(f)}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n}{
   \operatorname{Inf}_{j}(f)}\leq\sqrt{\max_{1\leq j\leq
   n}{\operatorname{Inf}_{j}(f)}}\,\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\sqrt{\operatorname{Inf}
   _{j}(f)}}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Following the notation of the proof of Theorem 1.1, it is clear that,
   if
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">S=\{d\}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><msup><mi>S</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">S^{\prime}=[d-1]</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   , then
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munderover><mo

   <mi>n</mi></munderover><msqrt><mrow><msub><mi>Inf</mi><mi>j</mi></msub>
   <mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></msqrt></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">≤</mo><mrow><munder><mo movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>∈</mo><mr
   ow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></munder><msup><mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.600em" minsize="1.600em">(</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   lspace="0em" movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>∈</mo><mr
   ow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>S</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>n
   </mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></munder><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>1</mn>
   </msub><mo>⊕</mo><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">)</mo></mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msu
   p></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\sqrt{\operatorname{Inf}_{j
   }(f)}}\leq\sum_{\mathbf{j}_{1}\in\mathcal{J}(S,n)}{\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{j
   }_{2}\in\mathcal{J}(S^{\prime},n)}{|a_{\mathbf{j}_{1}\oplus\mathbf{j}_{
   2}}|^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,,</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   and we already know from that proof that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>
   ∈</mo><mrow><mi
   class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></munder><msup><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em" maxsize="1.600em"

   movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>∈</mo><mr
   ow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒥</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>S</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>n
   </mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></munder><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>a</mi><mrow><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>1</mn>
   </msub><mo>⊕</mo><msub><mi>𝐣</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">)</mo></mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msu
   p></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>d</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>ρ</mi><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>−</
   mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"

   >2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi></msup></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\sum_{\mathbf{j}_{1}\in\mathcal{J}(S,n)}{\
   Big(\sum_{\mathbf{j}_{2}\in\mathcal{J}(S^{\prime},n)}{|a_{\mathbf{j}_{1
   }\oplus\mathbf{j}_{2}}|^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\binom{d}{1}\rho^{d
   -1}_{1}2d\leq d^{2}e^{d}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Hence, applying this estimation to (50) we get as desired
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>Var</mi><mo>⁡</mo><msup><mrow><
   mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo

   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>≤</mo><mrow><ms

   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{\operatorname{Var}(f)^{2}}{\max_{1\l
   eq j\leq n}{\operatorname{Inf}_{j}(f)}}\leq
   d^{4}e^{4d}\,.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

4.2. Lorentz norms

   From [12] we know that for each positive integer
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   the Lorentz space
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi
   mathvariant="normal">ℓ</mi><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\ell_{\frac{2d}{d+1},1}</annotation></sema
   ntics> :MATH]
   is the smallest symmetric Banach sequence space
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>X</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">X</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   for which there is a Bohnenblust-Hille inequality (replacing on the
   left-hand side of the inequality the
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi
   mathvariant="normal">ℓ</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\ell_{\frac{2d}{d+1}}</annotation></semant
   ics> :MATH]
   -norm by the
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>X</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">X</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -norm).

Theorem 4.2.

   There is
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">L\geq 1</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   such that every function
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   of degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   satisfies
   (51)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munderover><mo
   movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow>
   <mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></munderover><mrow><msup><mi>f</mi
   ><mo>∗</mo></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo
   lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>k</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d<
   /mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></msup></mfrac></mrow></mro
   w><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mi>d</mi></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annot
   ation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\sum_{k=1}^{D(n,d)}f^{*}(k)\,\frac{1}{k^{\
   frac{d-1}{2d}}}\leq L^{d}\,\|f\|_{\infty}\,,</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]

   where
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><msubsup><mo
   rspace="0em">∑</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mi>d</mi
   ></msubsup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac
   linethickness="0pt"><mi>n</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow
   ></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">D(n,d)=\sum_{m=0}^{d}\binom{n}{m}</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH: <semantics><msubsup><mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>f</mi><mo>∗</mo></msup><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mr
   ow><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\big(f^{*}(k)\big)_{k=1}^{D(n,d)}</annotat
   ion></semantics> :MATH]
   is the decreasing rearrangement of
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">(</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">)</mo></mrow><mtable
   rowspacing="0pt"><mtr><mtd><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⊂</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><mrow>
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mtd></mtr></
   mtable></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\big(|\widehat{f}(S)|\big)_{\begin{subarra
   y}{c}S\subset[n]\\ |S|\leq d\end{subarray}}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
    .

   Note that the norm on the left-hand side of (51) is the norm in the
   Lorentz space
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi
   mathvariant="normal">ℓ</mi><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.600em" minsize="1.600em">(</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>⊂</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo><mo fence="false"
   rspace="0.167em" stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>S</mi><mo fence="false"
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mo lspace="0.167em">≤</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em">(</mo><munder><mo lspace="0em"
   movablelimits="false">∏</mo><mtable
   rowspacing="0pt"><mtr><mtd><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⊂</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><mrow>
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mtd></mtr></
   mtable></munder><mi>ℝ</mi><mo>,</mo><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">∥</mo><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">⋅</mo><msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0.167em">∥</mo><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em"
   rspace="0.170em">)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\ell_{\frac{2d}{d+1},1}\Big(\{S\colon
   S\subset[n],|S|\leq
   d\}\Big)=\Big(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset[n]\\ |S|\leq
   d\end{subarray}}\mathbb{R},\|\cdot\|_{\frac{2d}{d+1},1}\Big)\,,</annota
   tion></semantics> :MATH]

   and hence this inequality generalizes the BH-inequality for functions
   on the Boolean cube – with the constraint that we do not know whether
   the constant in (51) still behaves subexponentially. If
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">C(d)</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   denotes the best possible constant in (51) (replacing
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mi>L</mi><mi>d</mi></msup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">L^{d}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   ), then the following proof shows that
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mo>lim sup</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mi>d</mi></mro
   w></msup></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msq
   rt></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\limsup_{d\rightarrow\infty}C(d)^{1/d}\leq
   \sqrt{2}(1+\sqrt{2})</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   .

Proof of Theorem 4.2.

   Let
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   be a polynomial of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . If
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   is the corresponding tetrahedral polynomial, then considered as a
   complex polynomial (of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   ) it follows from [12, Theorem 16] that for every
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>ε</mi><mo>></mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\varepsilon>0</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   there is a constant
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>ε</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">C(\varepsilon)</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   depending just on
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>ε</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\varepsilon</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   such that
   (52)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em"
   minsize="1.600em" stretchy="true">‖</mo><msub><mrow><mo
   maxsize="1.200em" minsize="1.200em">(</mo><mrow><mover
   accent="true"><mi>f</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo maxsize="1.200em"
   minsize="1.200em">)</mo></mrow><mtable
   rowspacing="0pt"><mtr><mtd><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⊂</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>n</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><mrow>
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>S</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mtd></mtr></
   mtable></msub><mo maxsize="1.600em" minsize="1.600em"
   stretchy="true">‖</mo></mrow><msub><mi
   mathvariant="normal">ℓ</mi><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn
   ></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></msub><mo>≤</mo><mro
   w><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>ε</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mi>ε</
   mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">sup</mo><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>𝔻</mi><mi>n</m
   i></msup></mrow></munder><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\Big\|\big(\widehat{f}(S)\big)_{\begin{sub
   array}{c}S\subset[n]\\ |S|\leq
   d\end{subarray}}\Big\|_{\ell_{\frac{2d}{d+1},1}}\leq
   C(\varepsilon)(\sqrt{2}+\varepsilon)^{d}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{D}^{n}}{|Q_{f
   }(z)|}.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   Note that the result in [12, Theorem 16] is claimed for
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous functions. But the result easily extends to the degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -case; indeed, every degree-
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   polynomial
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{f}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   on
   [MATH: <semantics><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">[-1,1]^{n}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   can be viewed as a
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   -homogeneous polynomial on
   [MATH:
   <semantics><msup><mi>𝕋</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><
   /msup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbb{T}^{n+1}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   with the the same complex norm and the same coefficients: consider
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>ω</mi><mo lspace="0.170em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>ω</mi><mo
   stretchy="true">¯</mo></mover></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mi>n</mi></
   msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mover
   accent="true"><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="true">¯</mo></mover></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\omega\,Q_{f}(z_{1}\overline{\omega},\ldot
   s,z_{n}\overline{\omega})</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . On the other hand, recall Klimek’s result from Lemma 1.3(4)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false">sup</mo><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>𝔻</mi
   ><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow></munder><mrow><mo lspace="0em"
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msq
   rt></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mi>d</mi></msup><mo
   lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munder><mo
   movablelimits="false"
   rspace="0em">sup</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></munder><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\sup_{z\in\mathbb{D}^{n}}{|Q_{f}(z)|}\leq(
   1+\sqrt{2})^{d}\,\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}{|Q_{f}(x)|}.</annotation></semantics
   > :MATH]

   Combining this with (52) we get (51). ∎

4.3. Norm estimates for homogeneous parts

   In view of Lemma 1.3,(4) and the difficulties described at the
   beginning of section 3 it would be interesting to know whether
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msqrt><
   mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">C=1+\sqrt{2}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   is the best constant satisfying
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></msub><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msup><mi>C</mi><mi>d
   </mi></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><mi>f</mi><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\|f_{m}\|_{\infty}\leq
   C^{d}\|f\|_{\infty}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   for every
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo lspace="0.278em"
   rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup><mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">f:\{\pm
   1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   of degree
   [MATH: <semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>d</mi></mr
   ow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq m\leq
   d</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   . See also the commend made at the end of section 3.
